ANNEXURE-XVII
View of JIPMER
Dr. C. Adithan: Remarks on THE NATIONAL COMMISSION FOR HUMAN RESOURCES FOR
HEALTH BILL, 2011
Preamble:
Central Government shall by notification establish the
• National Commission for Human Resources for Health with three constituent bodies, namely
• National Board of Health Education,
• National Evaluation and Assessment Committee
• National Councils,
with distinct responsibilities for regulating educational standards; enforcing the standards and assuring
quality and governing medical practice along ethical norms. It would also be the task of the
Commission to ensure effective use of linkages in the entire health system, act as the controlling and
coordinating agency that ensures accountability in the system and to facilitate interconnectivity among
and between disciplines to meet the needs of a diverse and growing health system in the country.
General Comment:
In general, it is a welcome initiative. The following are some suggestions for your kind consideration
CHAPTER II. National Commission for Human Resources for Health
Existing: Clause 7.  The Chairperson and other Members, shall immediately after entering office and
every year thereafter, make a declaration to the extent of their interest, whether direct or indirect and
whether financial or otherwise, in any health institution or any other institution which comes under
the purview of the Commission and the same shall be displayed on the website of the Commission
Suggestion: In addition to above, the Chairperson and other members should give declaration of
their and family members, immovable and movable properties at the time of assuming charge and
every year thereafter which will be displayed in the website of the commission.
Existing: Clause 8.  ( 1) The Chairperson and other Members, on ceasing to hold office shall not, for
a period of two years,  accept any employment (including as consultant or expert or otherwise) in
any health educational institution whose matter has been dealt with by such Chairperson or Member,
as the case may be, or has been before the Commission when he held office as such Chairperson
or Member.
Suggestion: The duration may be increased from 2 to 5 years.
Existing Clause 10.  ( 2) When the Chairperson is unable to discharge his functions owing to absence
on account of leave or otherwise, the seniormost whole-time Member shall discharge the functions of
the Chairperson till the Chairperson is able to discharge his functions
285

286
Suggestion: It may be included as follows: If the period of absence of Chairperson is less than 1
week, the acting Chairperson shall not take any policy decision or major financial decisions.
Clause 14.  No act or proceeding of the Commission shall be invalid merely by reason of-
(a) any vacancy in, or any defect in the constitution of, the Commission; or
(b) any defect in the appointment of a person as a Member of the Commission;
or
(c) any irregularity in the procedure of the Commission not affecting the merits of the case.
Comment: a debatable issue.1f the constitution of committee itself defective, how their decision will
be valid/acceptable?
Clause 17. ( 6) Where no order passed by the Commission has been communicated to the person
submitting the scheme, within a period of one year from the date of submission of such scheme
to the Commission under sub-section ( 2), the scheme shall be deemed to have been approved by the
Commission in the form in which it had been Submitted, and, accordingly, the permission of the
Commission required under sub-section ( 1) shall also be deemed to have been granted.
Comment: This may be removed. Each institution should get permission from the commission in
order to ensure the quality of medical education. In case of delay in getting response from the
commission, a mechanism or agency should be created/named to whom the affected Institution can
approach.
CHAPTER III: “National Board for Health Education” to assist the Commission in discharge of
its powers and functions for the purposes of health education
Clause 30.2.  (e) organise seminars, trainings or conferences for continuous medical education and
such other courses, workshops or programme as may be specified by regulations made by the
Commission;
(f) to undertake faculty development programmes;
Comment: Besides organizing the above, it should also provide financial support for organizing
seminars, trainings etc.
Clause 30.2 ( i) conduct examinations for entry to any under graduate, post graduate, doctoral, post
doctoral, super speciality or diploma courses, fellowship examination and screening test;
Query: Whether admission to PhD program in medical sciences is also included under doctoral
course? If so, is it advisable to have a national level entrance examn. for PhD admission?
Clause 33 ( 4) The Commission may, by notification, exempt any person, who possesses the
recognised qualification and enrolled as health practitioner in a country outside India for a period of
not less than three years, from the requirement of the screening test under this section.
Comment: The purpose is not clear. This may lead to malpractice.

287
CHAPTER IV: National Evaluation and Assessment Committee for evaluation and assessment
of any university or institution seeking permission
Clause 35.
(2) The President and members referred to in sub-section ( 1) shall be chosen from the persons of
eminence, integrity and outstanding calibre having post graduate degree in the discipline of health,
health policy, public health, management, technology, law or finance from a recognised university or
institution having ten years of experience in the respective field.
Comment: Why law or finance? They may be consulted/approached as and when needed. They need
not be members of this committee.
Clause 37 ( 4) Notwithstanding anything contained in this Act or any other law for the time being in
force, the Commission or Committee shall not engage the same evaluation and assessment agency
for successive inspections or audit inspections, to carry out audit or review inspection of any institute
at least for five years
Suggestion: It may be changed from “same evaluation and assessment agency” to “same evaluation
and assessment agency or persons”
Clause 40. ( 1) The Committee shall meet at least once in every three months, at such time and place
as it may appoint.
(2) The quorum of a meeting of the Committee shall be two members.
Suggestions: It may meet at least once in two months (considering their responsibility) and the
quorum should be at least three (Kindly note that the total number of members is 7 in this committee)

ANNEXURE-XVIII
Views of NIMHANS, Bangalore
Observations and Comments of the Director and Registrar of NIMHANS, Bangalore
on the National Commission for Human Resources for Health Bill, 2011
Ref No.: Your letter RS.11/1/2011– Com(H&FW) dated 23 rd July, 2012.
The efforts of Ministry of Health and Family Welfare, Government of India, to bring out an Over
Arching Body by an Act of Parliament and bring various systems of medicine under one umbrella –
A National Commission for Human Resource for Health (NCHRH), with accountability and regulating
educational standards is laudable and timely. It has envisaged to ensure effective usage of linkages in
the entire health system promoting interconnectivity between various disciplines. It is comprehensive
and is essential to maintain the identity of the health related field from other higher education and
research. This will facilitate taking steps from time to time to give direction and if necessary minor
midcourse corrections. The field of human resource for health is unique with close amalgamation of
health care delivery in the field, translational research in the centres of Higher Health Education and
innovation and exploration in the frontier areas. It has a strong component of social commitment
promoting positive mental health in contrast to phenomenological basic sciences research centres
attached to National Commission for Human Resources for Higher Education and Research.
The following observations of Director and Registrar of the National Institute of Mental Health And
Neurosciences (NlMHANS), Bangalore on the NCHRH bill are being submitted for the consideration
of Standing Parliamentary Committee on Health:
➢➢➢➢➢ 1. It may be worth considering naming it The National Commission for Human
Resources for Health Sciences Bill 2011, instead of The National Commission for
Human Resources for Health Bill.
➢➢➢➢➢ This is similar to University of Health Sciences and not University of Health. Calling
the Bill Human Resource for Health will be confined to health delivery to the society
realizing the shortage in the present state but it may in future cause stunted growth
in the broad field of health science research.
➢➢➢➢➢ The Health Sciences include many new technology intensive diagnostic and treatment
modalities which are essential for the day to day practice of medicine and patient
care. In the present suggested name, many of these go out of the ambit of health
related research and will be dominated by the Human Resources for Higher Education.
In the present form of the name the Health Research that may be carried out will
be mostly around Public Health and Social Sciences and Health Delivery but may not
emphasize translational innovations in technology. By naming it ‘Human Resources
for Health Sciences’ makes it broad and brings basic scientist and their innovations
into the ambit of Health Sciences and promote close interaction. This has helped the
evolution of great medical centres like Harward, Mass General Hospital, MD Anderson
Centre for Cancer, Memorial Sloan-Kettering Cancer Center etc., in USA offering
advanced medical treatment and at the same time promoting medical research. This
289

290
can be a productive marriage between Human Resources for Health Sciences and
Human Resources for Higher Education and Research. This, to an extent is reflected
in passing in page no.4, chapter 11, 3.4 participating in National Board for Health
Education, Evaluation and Assessment.
➢➢➢➢➢ 2. Page 4, Chapter II, 4.2, lines 44 - 48. Qualification for the Members-
➢➢➢➢➢ For the Member stipulating 20 years of experience in the profession out of which
atleast 15 years in the leadership role in the area of Health Care Delivery, Growth
and Development in Modern Medicine and Medical Education – can be a tall order.
It may be worth considering reducing to 10 years as Professor or Additional
Professor with leadership role for the Members. This can facilitate bringing younger
people familiar with modern technology to steer to future.3. Page 5,
➢➢➢➢➢ Chapter II, 4.2b – Eight part time members, each shall be appointed from the
disciplines of Medicine, Dentistry, Nursing, Pharmacy, Paramedics, Management,
Science and Technology and Law (the word each can be added for equitable
distributions to all the fields in a proactive way) and to avoid two members from
the same field finding a place.
➢➢➢➢➢ 4. Page No. 5, 1 b – Constitution of Selection Committee.
➢➢➢➢➢ Five other experts of eminence from the discipline of Medical and Allied Sciences
Management, Science and Technology having qualification and experience of at
least 20 years in the discipline as Members —
➢➢➢➢➢ It may be worth considering to reduce the experience for the Members to l5 years
to encourage younger people with modern thought and knowledge in newer
technologies to bring it to the point of care in disease management and offer new
ideas.
➢➢➢➢➢ 5 Page No. 14, 30.1 m,n,o,p,q, –
➢➢➢➢➢ It is worth considering to promote exchange of Visiting Professors from Institutions
of Higher Education and Research to Institutions of Health Research. This can be
indicated in one of the clauses. This will facilitate cooperation between Institutes for
Higher Education and Institutions of Health Research. This will promote strong inter
disciplinary interaction. The Biology divisions in IIT’s are carrying out advanced
health related research (IIT, Kanpur) leading to basic understanding of epilepsy, not
addressed in medical centres. The clinical material is shared for research with IIT
with informed consent of the patient.
➢➢➢➢➢ 6. Page 15, lines between 20 and 30, there is a mention of promoting Joint Cross
Disciplinary Programmes among Universities and other Educational Institutions.
Page 15, line 27 - 28, there is a mention of encouraging –
➢➢➢➢➢ Universities to enable Colleges to evolve into Health Institutions with power to award
Degrees – Does it include Basic Science Institutions like IIT’s starting Medical
Colleges. This needs to be clarified.
➢➢➢➢➢ 7. Page no.18, Chapter II, 39 – It is heartening to see a strong will to implement National
Evaluation and Assessment by establishing a Statutory Committee.

291
➢➢➢➢➢ 9. Page no. 41, Chapter VIII, Miscellaneous 118 (2)
➢➢➢➢➢ The Commission may transfer any officer or other employee or the services of such
officer to the Board, Committee and the National Council. This clause may be
applicable to Medical personnel who have gone abroad to update or require higher
education outside India when a doctor from Centres for Excellence goes abroad to
enhance the knowledge and comes back, will that person be posted to rural areas
to undertake service. Needs a comment and clarification when introduced in a Bill.
When doctors are transferred after a stint abroad, it should be to places where
reasonable facilities are available to practice the knowledge acquired and translate to
patient care. A clarification in this matter can be useful to avoid unrest among the
highly qualified people being posted to under developed zones and advising them to
participate in Health Delivery. This can lead to exodus to private sector or indifferent
work to complete the stipulated time and bring pressure to come back to well
equipped centres with the support of pressure groups in the Government. A strong
will is needed to counter it and ensure social equality.
➢➢➢➢➢ 10. Aspects not covered under the Bill:
➢➢➢➢➢ It may advisable to indicate that all the regional medical centres should have a
Department of Medical Biotechnology in addition to all other departments with
facility for training. The close proximaty of this department in a medical centre will
have positive effect in important training which is translatable. At present there are
many Institutions and Centres of Biotechnology bringing out course of student,
majority of them lacking basic aptitude for translational science. This department will
be distinct from a division of Biomedical Engineering in various centres who
essentially do maintenance works for the machines and their electronic components,
but no research.
➢➢➢➢➢ Every regional medical centres should have a separate Department of Disaster
Management and Critical Care. This will facilitate developing needed man power to
meet the natural and manmade calamities. Probably, trained man power in Disaster
Management in North Eastern part of India could have reduced the human suffering
by timely and proactive management. Once incorporate in the Bill training in this
field becomes mandatory and the country will have Rapid Action Medical Officers
in different zones of the country and reduce the pressure on Military and Paramilitary
Forces.
➢➢➢➢➢ Innovative approaches to teaching in Health Sciences especially Medical science is
not addressed anywhere in the bill. The bill should provide for exploring innovative
approaches to teaching which is gaining importance worldwide and emphasize on the
need for such novel approaches.

301
ANNEXURE-XX
Comments of MoHFW on the Oral Evidence given by various stakeholders before the Parliamentary Standing
Committee on the NCHRH Bill
AYUSH
Pharmacy
Council of India
(PCI)
Has supported the Ministry’s stand that AYUSH should not be a part of the NCHRH as :–
(i) They are completely two different streams of medicine and bringing them under one regulatory body, the
separate focus on AYUSH would be lost.
(ii) A YUSH needs to be propagated by having a separate standing.
(iii) Recognising that AYUSH needs to be developed more strongly and requires a separate focus, Department of
AYUSH was created 17-years back – bringing them together under one regulatory body would amount to
effectively reverting to pre-1995 status.
The Council has agreed with the vision of the NCHRH Bill and is not totally opposed to the proposal. However, it
has raised certain issues like:–
Issue
The new Council under
NCHRH would only maintain
the central register.
In the proposed Bill, there is
no mention about the
composition of State Councils.
Comment
The reconstituted Council will not only maintain the central register of pharmacists,
but also regulate the profession, renew the enrolment from time to time, grant
certificate of good standing and organise programmes for CME.
The present Councils like PCI are over-burdened with regulatory functions. The basic
objective of the NCHRH is to trifurcate the regulatory functions among three separate
bodies, viz. the National Board of Health Education, the National Evaluation and
Assessment Committee and the National Councils (like the PCI) to bring about and
accountability in the regulatory functions.
The Bill stipulates that where there are no State Councils, the State Government shall
constitute the State Councils within a period of three years from the date of
commencement of the NCHRH Act in such manner and with such composition as may
be prescribed by the State Government.

302
Issue
PCI is facing a lot of problems
because of dual regulation of
Pharmacy education by AICTE
and the Pharmacy Act. In
future also there will be a
conflict between NCHRH and
NCHER.
The Board does not have a
member from the field of
pharmacy.
Comment
After the setting up of NCHRH, all the allied fields of medical education like Pharmacy
would be brought under the ambit of the Commission and NCHER would only have
a say in matters of setting higher standards in these areas over and above the
minimum standards that would be notified by NCHRH.
The four part time members in NBHE will be from the discipline of health or health
education which includes Pharmacy.
Indian Nursing
Council
Central Council for
Indian Medicine
Central Council
of Homoeopathy
Medical Council of
India
The Council largely supports the Bill. However, it is of the opinion that the Bill should come after consultation and
discussions with the States.
Comments
The NCHRH Bill has been drafted after consulting all the stakeholders including the State Governments. Ministry of
Health and Family Welfare had held regional consultation on the proposal in six different cities wherein besides
representatives of the State Government, Vice Chancellors, Principals of medical colleges, medical practitioners,
academicians and representatives of regional IMA participated. The proposal was also unanimously approved by the
Central Council of Health and Family Welfare in the conference held on 30
th August, 2010.
The Council has supported the Ministry’s stand to keep AYUSH out of the purview of the NCHRH on the grounds
that both the streams have different pharmacopeia, concept and ideology.
The Council has also supported that AYUSH (Homoeopathy) should be kept out of the purview of NCHRH since
otherwise the stream of homoeopathy would lose its identity.
The Council supports the Bill and the idea that the regulatory functions should be trifurcated among three separate
bodies. The Council has, however, stressed the need for a strong selection committee for selection of members to
the Commission. The Council has also pointed out that NCHRH would provide cross connectivity between various
health disciplines leading to a synergistic approach to address the human resources needs of the health sector in the
country.

303
Dental Council of
India
Dr. R.C. Deka,
Director, AIIMS
The NCHRH would be totally
under the control of the
Central Government and none
of the States will have any
role to play in policy issues
relating to health.
Clause 100 of the Bill bars
challenging the decision of
the Commission in a court
of law.
NCHRH will have no elected
members.
The Bill gives absolute
autocratic powers to Central
Government under clause
115(2).
Issue
The Bill is not based on any
survey or study. The
Government or the Parliament
should constitute a committee
like Bhore Committee to find
out the need and deficiencies
for both in educational and
health care sectors.
The Central Council for Health and Family Welfare is an apex advisory body comprising
of all State Health Ministers of the country. NCHRH will be guided by a National
Policy for Human Resources for Health to be developed by public health leaders
representing State and Central Government through the Central Council for Health and
Family Welfare. The present Councils like the Medical Council of India and the Dental
Council of India are not guided by any such policy.
Insofar as provisions under clause 100 are concerned, it is stated that a Commission
or tribunal is established with limited powers to decide and investigate matters falling
under its ambit so that ordinary courts are not overburdened. However, such
Commissions/tribunals are amenable to constitutional courts, Therefore a person
aggrieved with the decision of the Commission can always file an appeal in the High
Court or the Supreme Court and his fundamental rights would remain intact. The bill
provides for an appellate authority in the Central Government.
The President of each of the National Councils shall be an ex-officio member of the
Commission; therefore the Commission would comprise of both nominated as well as
elected members.
The provision is required to be exercised by the Government in extraordinary
circumstances with a view to improve the overall functioning of the Commission/
Board/Committee/Council and bring in more transparency and accountability.
Comment
The Government had appointed a Task Force for setting up of the NCHRH in June,
2009 under the chairmanship of the then Union Health Secretary and 11 other
members. The task force. proposed a model for NCHRH as an overarching body
responsible for setting the mandate, coordinating and determining standards for health
and medical education, and providing an overall framework for the regulation of
human resources in health in the country. The report of the task force and the draft
NCHRH bill were placed on the website of MoHFW to invite comments and suggestions
of the stakeholders and general public.
Subsequently, the Ministry also held regional consultation on the proposal in six different
cities wherein besides representatives of the State Government, Vice Chancellors, Principals
of medical colleges, medical practitioners, academicians and representatives of regional

304
Comment
IMA participated. The proposal was also unanimously approved by the Central Council
of Health and Family Welfare in the conference held on 30 th August, 2010.
Lastly, the National Knowledge Commission and the Yashpal Committee have both
recommended reforms in medical education in the country. The High Level Expert Group
report on Universal Health Coverage of India has supported the setting up of NCHRH.
The problem started after 1993 probably because the private sector was allowed entry
into medical education and permitted to set up medical colleges.
The NCHRH Bill specifies the Acts which it seeks to repeal after being set up. The
institutions like AIIMS would continue to function under their respective Acts.
The composition of Selection Committee is laid down in the Bill and it shall consist
of persons of eminence and experience from medical and allied fields, to avoid any
bias in selection. Further, the Selection Committee shall only recommend a panel of
two names for every vacancy to the Central Government and the Central Government
shall make the final appointment.
The Bill clearly stipulates that NBHE would approve and notify minimum norms and
standards of maintenance of academic quality for accreditation and benchmarking of
education in recognised health educational institutions imparting approved courses in
the discipline of health throughout the period of accreditation. Nothing contained in the
NCHRH Bill will prevent NCHER or the concerned statutory regulatory body to specify
higher standards for health educational institutions.

opening of an institution.
There is no specific mention

Selection of people appears
too much Governmental. It
should be more autonomous.
The Bill does not empower
the universities or medical
schools which ultimately have
to make changes in
curriculum and practice.

The functions of NBHE inter alia  include taking such steps as may be desirable to

In so far as provisions under clause 100 are concerned, it is stated that a Commission
or tribunal is established with limited powers to decide and investigate matters falling
under its ambit so that ordinary courts are not overburdened. However, such
Commissions/tribunals are amenable to constitutional courts. Therefore a person
aggrieved with the decision of the Commission can always file an appeal in the High
Court or the Supreme Court and his fundamental rights would remain intact. The bill
provides for an appellate authority in the Central Government.
In the existing statutes, the punishment for contravening the provisions is non-
recognition of degree awarded to the student and not to the college itself, which does
not have sufficient deterrent value. Under the provisions of the NCHRH Act
contraventions will be punishable for a term which may not be less than 1 year and
may extend up to 3 years and a fine which may extend to Rs.25 lakhs.

The NCHRH will trifurcate the regulatory functions among three separate bodies, viz.
the National Board of Health Education, the National Evaluation and Assessment
Committee and the National Councils. The NBHE would determine, coordinate and
maintain standards for health education and also specify the minimum requirements for
faculty, infrastructure. and clinical work load for establishment of institutions. The
evaluation and assessment of health educational institutions shall be done by the
Evaluation and Assessment agencies having such qualification and experience and in
such manner as may be specified by regulations made by the Commission. The NEAC
shall take measures to develop and regulate the process of evaluation and assessment
of institutions imparting health education and programmes conducted therein, and
monitor the functioning of the agencies recommended by the Commission.
Any person can practice anywhere provided he is registered with the National Council.
The person enrolled with a State Council can practice in that State only. This is
necessary because under the current system, there is lot confusion and there are cases
where doctors other have multiple registration. The proposed system would enable the
Government to have an accurate picture of the number of practitioners in a given state
and to work out the distribution of human resources in health accordingly.
The Central Council for Health and Family Welfare is an apex advisory body comprising
of all State Health Ministers of the country. NCHRH will be guided by a National
Policy for Human Resources for Health to be developed by public health leaders
representing State and Central Government through the Central Council for Health and
Family Welfare. The present Councils like the Medical Council of India and the Dental
Council of India are not guided by any such policy.

self-governance of various
activities in certain professions.
There should be a greater
role for the States in the,
NCHRH.
Comment
The NCHRH Bill has been drafted after consulting all the stakeholders including the
State Governments. Ministry of Health and Family Welfare had held regional consultation
on the proposal in six different cities including Kolkata wherein besides representatives
of the State Government, Vice Chancellors, Principals of medical colleges, medical
practitioners, academicians and representatives of regional IMA participated. The minutes
of the meeting held in Kolkata has been furnished to the Committee. The proposal was
also unanimously approved by the Central Council of Health and Family Welfare in the
conference held on 30
th August, 2010.
The NCHRH does not seek to usurp any powers that currently vest with the State
Governments. At present Councils like the Medical Council of India, the Dental Council
of India and the Nursing Council of India, each created under a separate central
statute, regulate education and practice of profession in their respective fields. All these
Councils are autonomous in nature and State Governments do not have any specific
role in their functioning.
The existing Councils comprise of members elected by the professionals themselves
and nominated by the Central Government. The States do not play any role in the
constitution of these Councils, as well.
The Government is open to increasing representation of State Governments in some
appropriate form both in the Apex institution and in the NBHE and NEAC. It may also
kindly be noted that during the course of the debate in the Rajya Sabha on the recent
amendments to the Indian Medical Council Act, 1956 Hon'ble Health and Family
Welfare Minister assured the House, in response to a statement by an AIADMK
member, that he would hold detailed all party consultations before bringing the NCHRH
Bill back to the House, after it has been considered by the Department related
Parliamentary Standing Committee on Health and Family Welfare.

309
The State Government feels
that the bill attempts to create
a mechanism which is so
overarching that it is likely
to lead to delays, difficulties
and so on. The specific roles
of the existing Councils are
being broken into three and
therefore, coordination will
become difficult and lead to
delay in execution.
The Commission as well as
other bodies under it are
nominative in nature and is
no democratic element.
Commission is expected to
make assessment of
requirement of manpower in
order to ensure better
geographical spread, and
meeting the local needs. In this
situation the State Governments
have a role to play.
The present regulatory bodies like MCI and DCI are overburdened with all regulatory
functions with little time available for other major functions. Further, there is no cross
connectivity between various health disciplines which has led to uneven distribution of
health professionals in the country. Separate Councils each responsible for an important
cadre of health workers has fragmented the health educational sector from a policy
perspective and could not failed to provide a synergistic approach to address the
human resources needs of the health sector of the country.
The composition of the Commission and its constituent bodies are to be guided by
eminent persons not only from discipline of health education but also from other
disciplines like management, law etc. with a fresh and up to date outlook to enable
to respond to the societal, economic and aspirational changes in a manner that is in
cognisance with the current era.
Further, the President of each of the National Councils shall be an ex-officio member
of the Commission; therefore the Commission would comprise of both nominated as
well as elected members.
The Central Council for Health and Family Welfare is an apex advisory body comprising
of all State Health Ministers of the country. NCHRH will be guided by a National
Policy for Human Resources for Health to be developed by public health leaders
representing State and Central Government through the Central Council for Health and
Family Welfare. The present Councils like the Medical Council of India and the Dental
Council of India are not guided by any such policy.
Government of
Gujarat
Dr. K. Srinath
Reddy, National
Board of
Examinations (NBE)
The NCHRH Bill is welcome development. However, Dr. Reddy has raised the following issues;

310
Issue
The Bill does not define as
how on abolition of NBE the
DNB qualifications are going
to preserved, protected and
promoted within the ambit of
the Bill.
Clause 16(f) of the Bill says
that the Commission will
coordinate existing health care
infrastructure in Central and
State Governments for
utilisation thereof. However,
health care infrastructure is
not really the part of the
Commission’s remit because
it is supposed to deal with
health professional education
and standards thereof.
There is an overlap in the
functions of the Board and
the Committee.
Comment
Clause 26(e) of the NCHRH Bill inter-alia clearly stipulates that all matters and things
engaged to be done by, with or for the existing NBE shall be deemed to be engaged
to be done by with or for, the Board.
Statutes of the present regulatory bodies do not mandate planning and provisioning of
human resources in their respective streams and therefore human resource management
in the health sector remains a neglected area. The NCHRH is being set up with the
twin objective of strengthening and reforming the current regulatory framework and
also to enhance supply of skilled manpower as per the need. The Commission will be
guided by a National Policy for human resources for health to be developed by public
health leaders representing State and Central Governments through the Central Council
for Health and Family Welfare.
One of the primary functions of the Commission would be to determine and promote
human resources in discipline of health education and training commensurate with the
requirement of such resources in different States and Union Territories. In doing so,
the Commission has to coordinate with the existing health care infrastructure in Central
and State Governments for effective utilisation thereof.
The functions of the Board and Committee are well defined and there is no overlap.
The Board will approve and notify minimum norms and standards of maintenance of
academic quality for accreditation and benchmarking of education in health institutions.
The assessment by the Committee would be based on these norms.
Indian Association
of Physiotherapists,
Mumbai
Faculty of Dental
Supports the Bill. However, wants a separate Council for Physiotherapists.
Comments
The Bill has the provision to allow Government notify constitution of a National Council for any other discipline of
health other than those mentioned in the first schedule of the Bill.

311
They have welcomed the Bill and suggested that there should be no elected members in the Council but selected
members. Further, it has also been suggested that the Commission should be given ministerial powers and that
Commission should have people from law, administration etc.
Comments:
The Bill has penal provisions which would be attracted if certain provisions of the Bill are contravened. Further, the
Commission also has members from the disciplines of management, science and technology, public health and law.
